            $y\in B$ by \cref{union_iff,subseteq,times_tuple_elim}.
            Contradiction.
        \end{subproof}
        \begin{byCase}
        \caseOf{$y\mathrel{R}z$.}
            $x\mathrel{R}z$ by \cref{generated_well_ordering,foundational_ordering,transitive}.
            Follows by \cref{union_iff}.
        \caseOf{$y\not\mathrel{R}z$.}
            $(y,z)\in B\times\{c\}$ by \cref{union_iff}.
            Thus $y\in B$ and $z=c$ by \cref{times_tuple_elim,singleton_iff}.
            $x\in B$ by \cref{subseteq,times_tuple_elim}.
            Hence $(x,z)\in B\times\{c\}$ by \cref{times_tuple_intro,singleton_intro}.
            Follows by \cref{union_iff}.
        \end{byCase}
    \end{subproof}

    Show $S$ is asymmetric.
    \begin{subproof}
        It suffices to show that for all $x,y$ such that $x\mathrel{S}y$
            we have $y\not\mathrel{S}x$ by \cref{asymmetric}.
        Fix $x$.
        Fix $y$.
        Assume $x\mathrel{S}y$.
        Show $y\not\mathrel{S}x$.
        \begin{subproof}
            Suppose not.
            $x,y\in B$ by \cref{union_iff,generated_well_ordering,foundational_ordering,subseteq,times_tuple_elim}.
            Thus $(x,y),(y,x)\in R$ by \cref{union_iff,times_tuple_elim,singleton_iff}.
            Contradiction by \cref{generated_well_ordering,foundational_ordering,asymmetric}.
        \end{subproof}
    \end{subproof}

    Show $S$ is connex on $C$.
    \begin{subproof}
        It suffices to show that for all $x,y$ such that $x\in C$ and $y\in C$ and $x\neq y$
            we have $x\mathrel{S}y$ or $y\mathrel{S}x$ by \cref{connex}.
        Fix $x$.
        Fix $y$.
        Assume $x\in C$ and $y\in C$ and $x\neq y$.
        Follows by \cref{generated_well_ordering,foundational_ordering,connex,cons_iff,union_iff,times,pair_eq_iff}.
    \end{subproof}

    Show that for all $D$ such that $D\subseteq C$ and $D\neq\emptyset$
        there exists $d\in D$ such that for all $x\in D$ we have $d\mathrel{S}x$ or $d=x$.
    \begin{subproof}
        Fix $D$.
        Assume $D\subseteq C$ and $D\neq\emptyset$.
        \begin{byCase}
        \caseOf{$D\inter B\neq\emptyset$.}
            Take $d\in D\inter B$ such that for all $b\in D\inter B$
                we have $d\mathrel{R}b$ or $d=b$
                by \cref{generated_well_ordering,foundational_ordering,inter_lower_right}.
            $d\in D$ by \cref{inter_elim_left}.
            For all $x\in D$ we have $d\mathrel{S}x$ or $d=x$
                by \cref{subseteq,cons_iff,inter_intro,inter_elim_left,inter_elim_right,union_iff,times,pair_eq_iff}.
            Follows by assumption.
        \caseOf{$D\inter B=\emptyset$.}
            $D\subseteq\{c\}$ by \cref{subseteq,cons_iff,inter_intro,emptyset,singleton_iff}.
            Take $d$ such that $d\in D$ by \cref{nonempty_inhabited}.
            Then $d=c$ by \cref{subseteq,singleton_iff}.
            Thus $c\in D$.
            Hence $D=\{c\}$ by \cref{subseteq_antisymmetric,singleton_subset_intro}.
            Hence $c\in D$ and for all $x\in D$ we have $c=x$ by \cref{singleton_iff}.
            Follows by assumption.
        \end{byCase}
    \end{subproof}

    Thus $S$ is a foundational ordering on $C$ by \cref{foundational_ordering}.

    Show that for all $b\in B$ we have $\downward{S}{b}=\downward{R}{b}$.
    \begin{subproof}
        Fix $b\in B$.
        Show $\downward{S}{b}\subseteq\downward{R}{b}$.
        \begin{subproof}
            It suffices to show that every element of $\downward{S}{b}$ is an element of $\downward{R}{b}$
                by \cref{subseteq}.
            Fix $x\in\downward{S}{b}$.
            $x\mathrel{S}b$ by \cref{downward_closure}.
            Thus $x\mathrel{R}b$ by \cref{union_iff,times_tuple_elim,singleton_iff}.
            Follows by \cref{dom_intro,downward_closure}.
        \end{subproof}
        Show $\downward{R}{b}\subseteq\downward{S}{b}$.
        \begin{subproof}
            It suffices to show that every element of $\downward{R}{b}$ is an element of $\downward{S}{b}$
                by \cref{subseteq}.
            Fix $x\in\downward{R}{b}$.
            $x\mathrel{R}b$ by \cref{downward_closure}.
            Thus $x\mathrel{S}b$ by \cref{union_iff}.
            Follows by \cref{dom_intro,downward_closure}.
        \end{subproof}
        Follows by \cref{subseteq_antisymmetric}.
    \end{subproof}

    Show $\downward{S}{c}=B$.
    \begin{subproof}
        Show $\downward{S}{c}\subseteq B$.
        \begin{subproof}
            It suffices to show that every element of $\downward{S}{c}$ is an element of $B$
                by \cref{subseteq}.
            Fix $x\in\downward{S}{c}$.
            $x\mathrel{S}c$ by \cref{downward_closure}.
            Follows by \cref{union_iff,subseteq,times_tuple_elim}.
        \end{subproof}
        Show $B\subseteq\downward{S}{c}$.
        \begin{subproof}
            It suffices to show that every element of $B$ is an element of $\downward{S}{c}$
                by \cref{subseteq}.
            Fix $b\in B$.
            $(b,c)\in B\times\{c\}$ by \cref{times_tuple_intro,singleton_intro}.
            Thus $b\mathrel{S}c$ by \cref{union_iff}.
            Follows by \cref{dom_intro,downward_closure}.
        \end{subproof}
        Follows by \cref{subseteq_antisymmetric}.
    \end{subproof}

    For all $x\in C$ we have $f(\downward{S}{x})=x$
        by \cref{cons_iff,generated_well_ordering}.
    Follows by \cref{generated_well_ordering}.
\end{proof}

% from §13 helper lemma 6: an initial generated ordering is a subrelation
\begin{lemma}\label{common_initial_segment_relation_subseteq}
    Suppose $R$ is a well ordering generated by $f$ on $B$.
    Suppose $S$ is a well ordering generated by $f$ on $C$.
    Suppose $B$ is a common initial segment of $R$ on $B$ and $S$ on $C$.
    Then $R\subseteq S$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
    It suffices to show that every element of $R$ is an element of $S$ by \cref{subseteq}.
    Fix $w\in R$.
    Take $x,y$ such that $w=(x,y)$ by \cref{generated_well_ordering,foundational_ordering,subseteq,times_elem_is_tuple}.
    Then $x\mathrel{R}y$ and $y\in B$.
    Thus $x\in\downward{R}{y}$ by \cref{dom_intro,downward_closure}.
    $\downward{R}{y}=\downward{S}{y}$ by \cref{common_initial_segment}.
    Hence $x\mathrel{S}y$ by \cref{downward_closure}.
\end{proof}

% from §13 helper lemma 7: symmetry of common initial segments
\begin{lemma}\label{common_initial_segment_symmetric}
    Suppose $I$ is a common initial segment of $R$ on $B$ and $S$ on $C$.
    Then $I$ is a common initial segment of $S$ on $C$ and $R$ on $B$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
    Follows by \cref{common_initial_segment,inter_comm}.
\end{proof}

% from §13 helper lemma 8: the union of all generated stages
\begin{lemma}\label{union_generated_well_orderings}
    Suppose $G$ is inhabited.
    Suppose for all $p\in G$ we have $\snd{p}$ is a well ordering generated by $f$ on $\fst{p}$.
    Suppose $U=\{\fst{p}\mid p\in G\}$ and $V=\{\snd{p}\mid p\in G\}$.
    Suppose $B=\unions{U}$ and $S=\unions{V}$.
    Then $S$ is a well ordering generated by $f$ on $B$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
    Show that for all $p\in G$ we have for all $q\in G$
        $\snd{p}\subseteq\snd{q}$ or $\snd{q}\subseteq\snd{p}$.
    \begin{subproof}
        Fix $p\in G$.
        Fix $q\in G$.
        Follows by \cref{generated_well_orderings_compatible,common_initial_segment_symmetric,common_initial_segment_relation_subseteq}.
    \end{subproof}

    Show $S\subseteq B\times B$.
    \begin{subproof}
        It suffices to show that every element of $S$ is an element of $B\times B$ by \cref{subseteq}.
        Fix $w\in S$.
        Take $T\in V$ such that $w\in T$ by \cref{unions_iff}.
        Take $p\in G$ such that $T=\snd{p}$.
        $T\subseteq\fst{p}\times\fst{p}$ by \cref{generated_well_ordering,foundational_ordering}.
        $\fst{p}\in U$.
        Thus $\fst{p}\subseteq B$ by \cref{subseteq,unions_iff}.
        Hence $\fst{p}\times\fst{p}\subseteq B\times B$
            by \cref{times_subseteq_left,times_subseteq_right,subseteq_transitive}.
        Follows by \cref{subseteq}.
    \end{subproof}

    Show $S$ is transitive.
    \begin{subproof}
        It suffices to show that for all $x,y,z$ such that $x\mathrel{S}y\mathrel{S}z$
            we have $x\mathrel{S}z$ by \cref{transitive}.
        Fix $x$.
        Fix $y$.
        Fix $z$.
        Assume $x\mathrel{S}y\mathrel{S}z$.
        Take $p\in G$ such that $(x,y)\in\snd{p}$ by \cref{unions_iff}.
        Take $q\in G$ such that $(y,z)\in\snd{q}$ by \cref{unions_iff}.
        \begin{byCase}
        \caseOf{$\snd{p}\subseteq\snd{q}$.}
            $(x,y),(y,z)\in\snd{q}$ by \cref{subseteq}.
            Thus $(x,z)\in\snd{q}$ by \cref{generated_well_ordering,foundational_ordering,transitive}.
            Follows by \cref{unions_iff}.
        \caseOf{$\snd{q}\subseteq\snd{p}$.}
            $(x,y),(y,z)\in\snd{p}$ by \cref{subseteq}.
            Thus $(x,z)\in\snd{p}$ by \cref{generated_well_ordering,foundational_ordering,transitive}.
            Follows by \cref{unions_iff}.
        \end{byCase}
    \end{subproof}

    Show $S$ is asymmetric.
    \begin{subproof}
        It suffices to show that for all $x,y$ such that $x\mathrel{S}y$
            we have $y\not\mathrel{S}x$ by \cref{asymmetric}.
        Fix $x$.
        Fix $y$.
        Assume $x\mathrel{S}y$.
        Show $y\not\mathrel{S}x$.
        \begin{subproof}
            Suppose not.
            Take $p\in G$ such that $(x,y)\in\snd{p}$ by \cref{unions_iff}.
            Take $q\in G$ such that $(y,x)\in\snd{q}$ by \cref{unions_iff}.
            Either $\snd{p}\subseteq\snd{q}$ or $\snd{q}\subseteq\snd{p}$.
            Contradiction by \cref{subseteq,generated_well_ordering,foundational_ordering,asymmetric}.
        \end{subproof}
    \end{subproof}

    Show $S$ is connex on $B$.
    \begin{subproof}
        It suffices to show that for all $x,y$ such that $x\in B$ and $y\in B$ and $x\neq y$
            we have $x\mathrel{S}y$ or $y\mathrel{S}x$ by \cref{connex}.
        Fix $x$.
        Fix $y$.
        Assume $x\in B$ and $y\in B$ and $x\neq y$.
        Take $p\in G$ such that $x\in\fst{p}$ by \cref{unions_iff}.
        Take $q\in G$ such that $y\in\fst{q}$ by \cref{unions_iff}.
        \begin{byCase}
        \caseOf{$\fst{p}$ is a common initial segment of $\snd{p}$ on $\fst{p}$ and $\snd{q}$ on $\fst{q}$.}
            $\fst{p}\subseteq\fst{q}$ by \cref{common_initial_segment,inter_elim_right,subseteq}.
            Thus $x,y\in\fst{q}$ by \cref{subseteq}.
            Hence $x\mathrel{\snd{q}}y$ or $y\mathrel{\snd{q}}x$
                by \cref{generated_well_ordering,foundational_ordering,connex}.
            Follows by \cref{unions_iff}.
        \caseOf{$\fst{q}$ is a common initial segment of $\snd{p}$ on $\fst{p}$ and $\snd{q}$ on $\fst{q}$.}
            $\fst{q}\subseteq\fst{p}$ by \cref{common_initial_segment,inter_elim_left,subseteq}.
            Thus $x,y\in\fst{p}$ by \cref{subseteq}.
            Hence $x\mathrel{\snd{p}}y$ or $y\mathrel{\snd{p}}x$
                by \cref{generated_well_ordering,foundational_ordering,connex}.
            Follows by \cref{unions_iff}.
        \end{byCase}
    \end{subproof}

    Show that for all $D$ such that $D\subseteq B$ and $D\neq\emptyset$
        there exists $d\in D$ such that for all $x\in D$ we have $d\mathrel{S}x$ or $d=x$.
    \begin{subproof}
        Fix $D$.
        Assume $D\subseteq B$ and $D\neq\emptyset$.
        Take $x$ such that $x\in D$ by \cref{nonempty_inhabited}.
        Take $p\in G$ such that $x\in\fst{p}$ by \cref{subseteq,unions_iff}.
        $D\inter\fst{p}\neq\emptyset$ by \cref{inter_intro,emptyset}.
        Take $d\in D\inter\fst{p}$ such that for all $a\in D\inter\fst{p}$
            we have $d\mathrel{\snd{p}}a$ or $d=a$
            by \cref{generated_well_ordering,foundational_ordering,inter_lower_right}.
        $d\in D$ by \cref{inter_elim_left}.
        Show that for all $y\in D$ we have $d\mathrel{S}y$ or $d=y$.
        \begin{subproof}
            Fix $y\in D$.
            Take $q\in G$ such that $y\in\fst{q}$ by \cref{subseteq,unions_iff}.
            \begin{byCase}
            \caseOf{$\fst{p}$ is a common initial segment of $\snd{p}$ on $\fst{p}$ and $\snd{q}$ on $\fst{q}$.}
                Show $d\mathrel{S}y$ or $d=y$.
                \begin{subproof}
                    \begin{byCase}
                    \caseOf{$y\in\fst{p}$.}
                        $y\in D\inter\fst{p}$ by \cref{inter_intro}.
                        Thus $d\mathrel{\snd{p}}y$ or $d=y$.
                        Follows by \cref{unions_iff}.
                    \caseOf{$y\notin\fst{p}$.}
                        $d,y\in\fst{q}$ and $d\neq y$ by \cref{common_initial_segment,inter_elim_right,inter_elim_left,subseteq}.
                        $\downward{\snd{p}}{d}=\downward{\snd{q}}{d}$
                            by \cref{common_initial_segment,inter_elim_right}.
                        $\downward{\snd{p}}{d}\subseteq\fst{p}$
                            by \cref{common_initial_segment,inter_elim_right}.
                        Thus $d\mathrel{\snd{q}}y$ by \cref{generated_well_ordering,foundational_ordering,connex,dom_intro,downward_closure,subseteq}.
                        Follows by \cref{unions_iff}.
                    \end{byCase}
                \end{subproof}
            \caseOf{$\fst{q}$ is a common initial segment of $\snd{p}$ on $\fst{p}$ and $\snd{q}$ on $\fst{q}$.}
                $\fst{q}\subseteq\fst{p}$ by \cref{common_initial_segment,inter_elim_left,subseteq}.
                Thus $y\in D\inter\fst{p}$ by \cref{inter_intro,subseteq}.
                Hence $d\mathrel{\snd{p}}y$ or $d=y$.
                Follows by \cref{unions_iff}.
            \end{byCase}
        \end{subproof}
        Follows by assumption.
    \end{subproof}

    Thus $S$ is a foundational ordering on $B$ by \cref{foundational_ordering}.

    Show that for all $b\in B$ we have $f(\downward{S}{b})=b$.
    \begin{subproof}
        Fix $b\in B$.
        Take $p\in G$ such that $b\in\fst{p}$ by \cref{unions_iff}.
        Show $\downward{S}{b}=\downward{\snd{p}}{b}$.
        \begin{subproof}
            Show $\downward{S}{b}\subseteq\downward{\snd{p}}{b}$.
            \begin{subproof}
                It suffices to show that every element of $\downward{S}{b}$ is an element of $\downward{\snd{p}}{b}$
                    by \cref{subseteq}.
                Fix $x\in\downward{S}{b}$.
                Take $q\in G$ such that $(x,b)\in\snd{q}$ by \cref{downward_closure,unions_iff}.
                \begin{byCase}
                \caseOf{$\fst{p}$ is a common initial segment of $\snd{p}$ on $\fst{p}$ and $\snd{q}$ on $\fst{q}$.}
                    $\downward{\snd{p}}{b}=\downward{\snd{q}}{b}$ by \cref{common_initial_segment}.
                    $x\in\downward{\snd{q}}{b}$ by \cref{dom_intro,downward_closure}.
                    Follows by assumption.
                \caseOf{$\fst{q}$ is a common initial segment of $\snd{p}$ on $\fst{p}$ and $\snd{q}$ on $\fst{q}$.}
                    $\snd{q}\subseteq\snd{p}$ by \cref{common_initial_segment_symmetric,common_initial_segment_relation_subseteq}.
                    Thus $(x,b)\in\snd{p}$ by \cref{subseteq}.
                    Follows by \cref{dom_intro,downward_closure}.
                \end{byCase}
            \end{subproof}
            Show $\downward{\snd{p}}{b}\subseteq\downward{S}{b}$.
            \begin{subproof}
                It suffices to show that every element of $\downward{\snd{p}}{b}$ is an element of $\downward{S}{b}$
                    by \cref{subseteq}.
                Fix $x\in\downward{\snd{p}}{b}$.
                $(x,b)\in\snd{p}$ by \cref{downward_closure}.
                Thus $(x,b)\in S$ by \cref{unions_iff}.
                Follows by \cref{dom_intro,downward_closure}.
            \end{subproof}
            Follows by \cref{subseteq_antisymmetric}.
        \end{subproof}
        Follows by \cref{generated_well_ordering}.
    \end{subproof}

    Follows by \cref{generated_well_ordering}.
\end{proof}

% from §13 helper lemma 9: Hausdorff's maximal-chain principle
\begin{lemma}\label{hausdorff_maximal_chain}
    Suppose $Q$ is a binary relation on $P$.
    Suppose $Q$ is transitive and asymmetric.
    Then there exists $C$ such that $C$ is a maximal chain in $P$ with respect to $Q$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
    Suppose not.
    Let $H=\{C\in\pow{P}\mid \text{$C$ is a chain in $P$ with respect to $Q$}\}$.
    $\emptyset\in H$ by \cref{powerset_bottom,chain_in_relation,emptyset,emptyset_subseteq}.
    Thus $H$ is inhabited.

    Show that for all $C\in H$ there exists $p\in P\setminus C$ such that $\cons{p}{C}\in H$.
    \begin{subproof}
        Fix $C\in H$.
        $C$ is a chain in $P$ with respect to $Q$.
        $C$ is not a maximal chain in $P$ with respect to $Q$.
        Take $D$ such that $C\subseteq D$ and $D$ is a chain in $P$ with respect to $Q$ and $D\neq C$
            by \cref{maximal_chain_in_relation}.
        $C\subset D$.
        Take $p\in D$ such that $p\notin C$ by \cref{subset_witness}.
        $p\in P\setminus C$ by \cref{chain_in_relation,subseteq,setminus}.
        $\cons{p}{C}\subseteq D$ by \cref{cons_subseteq_intro}.
        Thus $\cons{p}{C}$ is a chain in $P$ with respect to $Q$
            by \cref{chain_in_relation,subseteq}.
        $\cons{p}{C}\subseteq P$ by \cref{chain_in_relation}.
        Hence $\cons{p}{C}\in H$ by \cref{pow_iff}.
        Follows by assumption.
    \end{subproof}

    Let $T=\{w\in H\times P\mid \text{$\snd{w}\notin\fst{w}$ and $\cons{\snd{w}}{\fst{w}}\in H$}\}$.
    $T\subseteq H\times P$.
    $T$ is a relation by \cref{relation,subseteq,times_elem_is_tuple}.
    Show that for all $C\in H$ there exists $p$ such that $C\mathrel{T}p$.
    \begin{subproof}
        Fix $C\in H$.
        Take $p\in P\setminus C$ such that $\cons{p}{C}\in H$.
        $(C,p)\in H\times P$ by \cref{setminus,times_tuple_intro}.
        $\fst{(C,p)}=C$ and $\snd{(C,p)}=p$ by \cref{fst_eq,snd_eq}.
        Thus $(C,p)\in T$.
        Follows by assumption.
    \end{subproof}
    Take $f$ such that $f$ is a function on $H$ and for all $C\in H$ we have $C\mathrel{T}f(C)$
        by \cref{axiom_of_choice}.
    Show that for all $C\in H$ we have $f(C)\in P\setminus C$ and $\cons{f(C)}{C}\in H$.
    \begin{subproof}
        Fix $C\in H$.
        $(C,f(C))\in T$.
        $\fst{(C,f(C))}=C$ and $\snd{(C,f(C))}=f(C)$ by \cref{fst_eq,snd_eq}.
        $f(C)\notin C$ and $\cons{f(C)}{C}\in H$.
        $f(C)\in P$ by \cref{subseteq,times_tuple_elim}.
        Follows by \cref{setminus}.
    \end{subproof}

    Let $G=\{p\in H\times\pow{P\times P}\mid \text{$\snd{p}$ is a well ordering generated by $f$ on $\fst{p}$}\}$.
    $(\emptyset,\emptyset)\in G$
        by \cref{times_tuple_intro,powerset_bottom,empty_generated_well_ordering,fst_eq,snd_eq}.
    Thus $G$ is inhabited.
    For all $p\in G$ we have $\snd{p}$ is a well ordering generated by $f$ on $\fst{p}$.

    Let $U=\{\fst{p}\mid p\in G\}$.
    Let $V=\{\snd{p}\mid p\in G\}$.
    Let $B=\unions{U}$.
    Let $S=\unions{V}$.
    $S$ is a well ordering generated by $f$ on $B$ by \cref{union_generated_well_orderings}.

    Show $B$ is a chain in $P$ with respect to $Q$.
    \begin{subproof}
        Show $B\subseteq P$.
        \begin{subproof}
            It suffices to show that every element of $B$ is an element of $P$ by \cref{subseteq}.
            Fix $x\in B$.
            Take $C\in U$ such that $x\in C$ by \cref{unions_iff}.
            Take $p\in G$ such that $C=\fst{p}$.
            $p\in H\times\pow{P\times P}$.
            Thus $C\in H$ by \cref{fst_type}.
            $C\subseteq P$ by \cref{chain_in_relation}.
            Follows by \cref{subseteq}.
        \end{subproof}
        Show that for all $x\in B$ we have for all $y\in B$
            $x=y$ or $x\mathrel{Q}y$ or $y\mathrel{Q}x$.
        \begin{subproof}
            Fix $x\in B$.
            Fix $y\in B$.
            Take $p\in G$ such that $x\in\fst{p}$ by \cref{unions_iff}.
            Take $q\in G$ such that $y\in\fst{q}$ by \cref{unions_iff}.
            \begin{byCase}
                \caseOf{$\fst{p}$ is a common initial segment of $\snd{p}$ on $\fst{p}$ and $\snd{q}$ on $\fst{q}$.}
                    $\fst{p}\subseteq\fst{q}$
                        by \cref{common_initial_segment,inter_elim_right,subseteq}.
                    Thus $x,y\in\fst{q}$ by \cref{subseteq}.
                    $q\in H\times\pow{P\times P}$.
                    Hence $\fst{q}\in H$ by \cref{fst_type}.
                    Thus $\fst{q}$ is a chain in $P$ with respect to $Q$.
                    Follows by \cref{chain_in_relation}.
                \caseOf{$\fst{q}$ is a common initial segment of $\snd{p}$ on $\fst{p}$ and $\snd{q}$ on $\fst{q}$.}
                    $\fst{q}\subseteq\fst{p}$
                        by \cref{common_initial_segment,inter_elim_left,subseteq}.
                    Thus $x,y\in\fst{p}$ by \cref{subseteq}.
                    $p\in H\times\pow{P\times P}$.
                    Hence $\fst{p}\in H$ by \cref{fst_type}.
                    Thus $\fst{p}$ is a chain in $P$ with respect to $Q$.
                    Follows by \cref{chain_in_relation}.
            \end{byCase}
        \end{subproof}
        Follows by \cref{chain_in_relation}.
    \end{subproof}

    $B\subseteq P$ by \cref{chain_in_relation}.
    Hence $B\in\pow{P}$ by \cref{pow_iff}.
    Thus $B\in H$.
    Let $c=f(B)$.
    $c\in P\setminus B$ and $\cons{c}{B}\in H$.
    Let $C=\cons{c}{B}$.
    Let $R=S\union(B\times\{c\})$.
    $R$ is a well ordering generated by $f$ on $C$
        by \cref{generated_well_ordering_adjoin,setminus}.
    $C\in H$.
    Hence $C\in\pow{P}$.
    Thus $C\subseteq P$ by \cref{pow_iff}.
    $R\subseteq C\times C$ by \cref{generated_well_ordering,foundational_ordering}.
    $C\times C\subseteq P\times P$
        by \cref{times_subseteq_left,times_subseteq_right,subseteq_transitive}.
    Hence $R\subseteq P\times P$ by \cref{subseteq_transitive}.
    Thus $R\in\pow{P\times P}$ by \cref{pow_iff}.
    $(C,R)\in H\times\pow{P\times P}$ by \cref{times_tuple_intro}.
    $\fst{(C,R)}=C$ and $\snd{(C,R)}=R$ by \cref{fst_eq,snd_eq}.
    Hence $(C,R)\in G$.
    Thus $C\in U$.
    Hence $C\subseteq B$ by \cref{subseteq,unions_iff}.
    Therefore $c\in B$ by \cref{cons_left,subseteq}.
    Contradiction by \cref{setminus}.
\end{proof}

% from §13 helper lemma 10: finite subsets of the union of an inclusion chain lie in one member
\begin{lemma}\label{finite_subset_of_chain_union}
    Suppose $M$ is inhabited.
    Suppose for all $C\in M$ we have for all $D\in M$ we have $C\subseteq D$ or $D\subseteq C$.
    Suppose $F$ is finite and $F\subseteq\unions{M}$.
    Then there exists $E\in M$ such that $F\subseteq E$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
    Let $K=\{Y\in\pow{F}\mid \text{there exists $E\in M$ such that $Y\subseteq E$}\}$.
    Take $E$ such that $E\in M$.
    $\emptyset\in\pow{F}$ by \cref{powerset_bottom}.
    $\emptyset\subseteq E$ by \cref{emptyset_subseteq}.
    Thus $\emptyset\in K$.

    Show that for all $Y,y$ such that $Y\in K$ and $y\in F$ we have $\cons{y}{Y}\in K$.
    \begin{subproof}
        Fix $Y$.
        Fix $y$.
        Assume $Y\in K$ and $y\in F$.
        $Y\in\pow{F}$.
        Hence $Y\subseteq F$ by \cref{pow_iff}.
        Thus $\cons{y}{Y}\subseteq F$ by \cref{cons_subseteq_intro}.
        Hence $\cons{y}{Y}\in\pow{F}$ by \cref{pow_iff}.
        Take $C\in M$ such that $Y\subseteq C$.
        $y\in\unions{M}$ by \cref{subseteq}.
        Take $D\in M$ such that $y\in D$ by \cref{unions_iff}.
        \begin{byCase}
            \caseOf{$C\subseteq D$.}
                $Y\subseteq D$ by \cref{subseteq_transitive}.
                Hence $\cons{y}{Y}\subseteq D$ by \cref{cons_subseteq_intro}.
                Thus $\cons{y}{Y}\in K$.
            \caseOf{$D\subseteq C$.}
                $y\in C$ by \cref{subseteq}.
                Hence $\cons{y}{Y}\subseteq C$ by \cref{cons_subseteq_intro}.
                Thus $\cons{y}{Y}\in K$.
        \end{byCase}
    \end{subproof}

    $F\in K$ by \cref{finite_induction}.
    Follows by definition.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}\label{zorn_subset_family}
    Assume $A\subseteq\pow{X}$.
    Suppose $A$ has the finite intersection property.
    Then there exists $D$ such that
        $A\subseteq D$ and
        $D$ is maximal with respect to the finite intersection property on $X$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
    Let $P=\{E\in\pow{\pow{X}}\mid \text{$A\subseteq E$ and $E$ has the finite intersection property}\}$.
    $A\in\pow{\pow{X}}$ by \cref{pow_iff}.
    $A\subseteq A$ by \cref{subseteq_refl}.
    Thus $A\in P$.
    Hence $P$ is inhabited.

    Let $Q=\{w\in P\times P\mid \text{$\fst{w}\subset\snd{w}$}\}$.
    $Q\subseteq P\times P$.
    Thus $Q$ is a binary relation on $P$.

    Show that for all $E,F$ such that $E\in P$ and $F\in P$ and $E\subset F$
        we have $E\mathrel{Q}F$.
    \begin{subproof}
        Fix $E$.
        Fix $F$.
        Assume $E\in P$ and $F\in P$ and $E\subset F$.
        $(E,F)\in P\times P$ by \cref{times_tuple_intro}.
        $\fst{(E,F)}=E$ and $\snd{(E,F)}=F$ by \cref{fst_eq,snd_eq}.
        Thus $(E,F)\in Q$.
    \end{subproof}

    Show $Q$ is transitive.
    \begin{subproof}
        It suffices to show that for all $E,F,K$ such that $E\mathrel{Q}F\mathrel{Q}K$
            we have $E\mathrel{Q}K$ by \cref{transitive}.
        Fix $E$.
        Fix $F$.
        Fix $K$.
        Assume $E\mathrel{Q}F\mathrel{Q}K$.
        $(E,F),(F,K)\in Q$.
        Hence $(E,F),(F,K)\in P\times P$.
        Thus $E,F,K\in P$ by \cref{times_tuple_elim}.
        $E\subset F$ and $F\subset K$ by \cref{fst_eq,snd_eq}.
        Hence $E\subseteq F\subseteq K$ by \cref{subset}.
        Thus $E\subseteq K$ by \cref{subseteq_transitive}.
        Take $x\in F$ such that $x\notin E$ by \cref{subset_witness}.
        $x\in K$ by \cref{subset,subseteq}.
        Hence $E\neq K$.
        Thus $E\subset K$ by \cref{subset}.
        Follows by assumption.
    \end{subproof}

    Show $Q$ is asymmetric.
    \begin{subproof}
        It suffices to show that for all $E,F$ such that $E\mathrel{Q}F$
            we have $F\not\mathrel{Q}E$ by \cref{asymmetric}.
        Fix $E$.
        Fix $F$.
        Assume $E\mathrel{Q}F$.
        Show $F\not\mathrel{Q}E$.
        \begin{subproof}
            Suppose not.
            $E\subset F$ and $F\subset E$ by \cref{fst_eq,snd_eq}.
            Hence $E\subseteq F\subseteq E$ by \cref{subset}.
            Thus $E=F$ by \cref{subseteq_antisymmetric}.
            Contradiction by \cref{subset_irrefl}.
        \end{subproof}
    \end{subproof}

    Take $M$ such that $M$ is a maximal chain in $P$ with respect to $Q$
        by \cref{hausdorff_maximal_chain}.
    $M$ is a chain in $P$ with respect to $Q$ by \cref{maximal_chain_in_relation}.
    Hence $M\subseteq P$ by \cref{chain_in_relation}.

    Show $M$ is inhabited.
    \begin{subproof}
        Suppose not.
        Show $M\subseteq\emptyset$.
        \begin{subproof}
            It suffices to show that every element of $M$ is an element of $\emptyset$ by \cref{subseteq}.
            Fix $x\in M$.
            Contradiction.
        \end{subproof}
        $\emptyset\subseteq M$ by \cref{emptyset_subseteq}.
        Hence $M=\emptyset$ by \cref{subseteq_antisymmetric}.
        Let $C=\cons{A}{M}$.
        $C=\{A\}$ by assumption.
        $C\subseteq P$ by \cref{singleton_subset_intro}.
        Show that for all $E\in C$ we have for all $F\in C$
            $E=F$ or $E\mathrel{Q}F$ or $F\mathrel{Q}E$.
        \begin{subproof}
            Fix $E\in C$.
            Fix $F\in C$.
            $E=A$ and $F=A$ by \cref{singleton_iff}.
            Thus $E=F$.
        \end{subproof}
        Hence $C$ is a chain in $P$ with respect to $Q$ by \cref{chain_in_relation}.
        $M\subseteq C$ by \cref{subseteq_cons_self}.
        Thus $C=M$ by \cref{maximal_chain_in_relation}.
        $A\in C$ by \cref{cons_left}.
        Hence $A\in M$.
        Contradiction by \cref{emptyset}.
    \end{subproof}

    Let $D=\unions{M}$.
    Show that for all $E\in M$ we have $E\subseteq D$.
    \begin{subproof}
        Fix $E\in M$.
        Follows by \cref{subseteq,unions_iff}.
    \end{subproof}
    Show that for all $E\in M$ we have for all $F\in M$
        $E\subseteq F$ or $F\subseteq E$.
    \begin{subproof}
        Fix $E\in M$.
        Fix $F\in M$.
        $E=F$ or $E\mathrel{Q}F$ or $F\mathrel{Q}E$ by \cref{chain_in_relation}.
        Follows by \cref{subseteq_refl,subset,fst_eq,snd_eq}.
    \end{subproof}

    Show $A\subseteq D$.
    \begin{subproof}
        It suffices to show that every element of $A$ is an element of $D$ by \cref{subseteq}.
        Fix $x\in A$.
        Take $E$ such that $E\in M$.
        $E\in P$ by \cref{subseteq}.
        Hence $A\subseteq E$.
        Thus $x\in E$ by \cref{subseteq}.
        Follows by \cref{unions_iff}.
    \end{subproof}

    Show $D\subseteq\pow{X}$.
    \begin{subproof}
        Show that for all $E\in M$ we have $E\subseteq\pow{X}$.
        \begin{subproof}
            Fix $E\in M$.
            $E\in P$ by \cref{subseteq}.
            Hence $E\in\pow{\pow{X}}$.
            Follows by \cref{pow_iff}.
        \end{subproof}
        Follows by \cref{unions_family}.
    \end{subproof}

    Show $D$ has the finite intersection property.
    \begin{subproof}
        $A$ is inhabited by \cref{finite_intersection_property}.
        Hence $D$ is inhabited by \cref{subseteq}.
        Show that for all $F$ such that $F\subseteq D$ and $F$ is finite and inhabited
            we have $\inters{F}\neq\emptyset$.
        \begin{subproof}
            Fix $F$.
            Assume $F\subseteq D$ and $F$ is finite and inhabited.
            Take $E\in M$ such that $F\subseteq E$ by \cref{finite_subset_of_chain_union}.
            $E\in P$ by \cref{subseteq}.
            Hence $E$ has the finite intersection property.
            Follows by \cref{finite_intersection_property}.
        \end{subproof}
        Follows by \cref{finite_intersection_property}.
    \end{subproof}

    $D\in\pow{\pow{X}}$ by \cref{pow_iff}.
    Hence $D\in P$.

    Show that for all $E\in M$ we have $E=D$ or $E\mathrel{Q}D$.
    \begin{subproof}
        Fix $E\in M$.
        $E\subseteq D$.
        \begin{byCase}
            \caseOf{$E=D$.}
                Follows by assumption.
            \caseOf{$E\neq D$.}
                $E\subset D$ by \cref{subset}.
                $E\in P$ by \cref{subseteq}.
                Thus $E\mathrel{Q}D$ by assumption.
        \end{byCase}
    \end{subproof}

    Let $C=\cons{D}{M}$.
    $C\subseteq P$ by \cref{cons_subseteq_intro}.
    Show that for all $E\in C$ we have for all $F\in C$
        $E=F$ or $E\mathrel{Q}F$ or $F\mathrel{Q}E$.
    \begin{subproof}
        Fix $E\in C$.
        Fix $F\in C$.
        \begin{byCase}
            \caseOf{$E=D$.}
                \begin{byCase}
                    \caseOf{$F=D$.}
                        Thus $E=F$.
                    \caseOf{$F\in M$.}
                        $F=D$ or $F\mathrel{Q}D$.
                        Follows by assumption.
                \end{byCase}
            \caseOf{$E\in M$.}
                \begin{byCase}
                    \caseOf{$F=D$.}
                        $E=D$ or $E\mathrel{Q}D$.
                        Follows by assumption.
                    \caseOf{$F\in M$.}
                        Follows by \cref{chain_in_relation}.
                \end{byCase}
        \end{byCase}
    \end{subproof}
    Hence $C$ is a chain in $P$ with respect to $Q$ by \cref{chain_in_relation}.
    $M\subseteq C$ by \cref{subseteq_cons_self}.
    Thus $C=M$ by \cref{maximal_chain_in_relation}.
    $D\in C$ by \cref{cons_left}.
    Hence $D\in M$.

    Show that for all $E$ such that $D\subseteq E\subseteq\pow{X}$ and
        $E$ has the finite intersection property we have $E=D$.
    \begin{subproof}
        Fix $E$.
        Assume $D\subseteq E\subseteq\pow{X}$ and $E$ has the finite intersection property.
        $A\subseteq E$ by \cref{subseteq_transitive}.
        $E\in\pow{\pow{X}}$ by \cref{pow_iff}.
        Hence $E\in P$.

        Show that for all $F\in M$ we have $F=E$ or $F\mathrel{Q}E$.
        \begin{subproof}
            Fix $F\in M$.
            $F\subseteq D\subseteq E$.
            Hence $F\subseteq E$ by \cref{subseteq_transitive}.
            \begin{byCase}
                \caseOf{$F=E$.}
                    Follows by assumption.
                \caseOf{$F\neq E$.}
                    $F\subset E$ by \cref{subset}.
                    $F\in P$ by \cref{subseteq}.
                    Thus $F\mathrel{Q}E$ by assumption.
            \end{byCase}
        \end{subproof}

        Let $K=\cons{E}{M}$.
        $K\subseteq P$ by \cref{cons_subseteq_intro}.
        Show that for all $F\in K$ we have for all $G\in K$
            $F=G$ or $F\mathrel{Q}G$ or $G\mathrel{Q}F$.
        \begin{subproof}
            Fix $F\in K$.
            Fix $G\in K$.
            \begin{byCase}
                \caseOf{$F=E$.}
                    \begin{byCase}
                        \caseOf{$G=E$.}
                            Thus $F=G$.
                        \caseOf{$G\in M$.}
                            $G=E$ or $G\mathrel{Q}E$.
                            Follows by assumption.
                    \end{byCase}
                \caseOf{$F\in M$.}
                    \begin{byCase}
                        \caseOf{$G=E$.}
                            $F=E$ or $F\mathrel{Q}E$.
                            Follows by assumption.
                        \caseOf{$G\in M$.}
                            Follows by \cref{chain_in_relation}.
                    \end{byCase}
            \end{byCase}
        \end{subproof}
        Hence $K$ is a chain in $P$ with respect to $Q$ by \cref{chain_in_relation}.
        $M\subseteq K$ by \cref{subseteq_cons_self}.
        Thus $K=M$ by \cref{maximal_chain_in_relation}.
        $E\in K$ by \cref{cons_left}.
        Hence $E\in M$.
        Thus $E\subseteq D$.
        Follows by \cref{subseteq_antisymmetric}.
    \end{subproof}

    $D$ is maximal with respect to the finite intersection property on $X$
        by \cref{maximal_finite_intersection_property_on}.
    Follows by assumption.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}\label{simple_order_relation}
    $R$ is a simple order relation on $X$ iff
    $R$ is a binary relation on $X$ and
    $R$ is transitive and
    $R$ is asymmetric and
    $R$ is connex on $X$.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}\label{smallest_element}
    $a$ is a smallest element of $X$ with respect to $R$ iff
    $a\in X$ and for all $x\in X$ we have $a\mathrel{R}x$ or $a = x$.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}\label{well_order_relation}
    $R$ is a well order relation on $X$ iff
        $R$ is a simple order relation on $X$ and
        for all $A$ such that $A\subseteq X$ and $A\neq\emptyset$
            there exists $a$ such that $a$ is a smallest element of $A$ with respect to $R$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}\label{well_ordering_theorem}
    There exists $R$ such that $R$ is a well order relation on $A$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
    Let $H=\{B\in\pow{A}\mid B\neq A\}$.
    Show that for all $B\in H$ there exists $a\in A\setminus B$.
    \begin{subproof}
        Fix $B\in H$.
        $B\in\pow{A}$ and $B\neq A$.
        Hence $B\subseteq A$ by \cref{pow_iff}.
        Thus $B\subset A$ by \cref{subset}.
        Take $a\in A$ such that $a\notin B$ by \cref{subset_witness}.
        Hence $a\in A\setminus B$ by \cref{setminus}.
        Follows by assumption.
    \end{subproof}

    Let $T=\{w\in H\times A\mid \snd{w}\notin\fst{w}\}$.
    $T\subseteq H\times A$.
    Hence $T$ is a relation by \cref{relation,subseteq,times_elem_is_tuple}.
    Show that for all $B\in H$ there exists $a$ such that $B\mathrel{T}a$.
    \begin{subproof}
        Fix $B\in H$.
        Take $a$ such that $a\in A\setminus B$.
        $(B,a)\in H\times A$ by \cref{setminus,times_tuple_intro}.
        $\fst{(B,a)}=B$ and $\snd{(B,a)}=a$ by \cref{fst_eq,snd_eq}.
        Hence $(B,a)\in T$ by \cref{setminus}.
        Follows by assumption.
    \end{subproof}
    Take $f$ such that $f$ is a function on $H$ and for all $B\in H$ we have $B\mathrel{T}f(B)$
        by \cref{axiom_of_choice}.
    Show that for all $B\in H$ we have $f(B)\in A\setminus B$.
    \begin{subproof}
        Fix $B\in H$.
        $(B,f(B))\in T$.
        Hence $(B,f(B))\in H\times A$.
        Thus $f(B)\in A$ by \cref{times_tuple_elim}.
        $\fst{(B,f(B))}=B$ and $\snd{(B,f(B))}=f(B)$ by \cref{fst_eq,snd_eq}.
        Hence $f(B)\notin B$.
        Follows by \cref{setminus}.
    \end{subproof}

    Let $G=\{p\in\pow{A}\times\pow{A\times A}\mid
        \text{$\snd{p}$ is a well ordering generated by $f$ on $\fst{p}$}\}$.
    $(\emptyset,\emptyset)\in G$
        by \cref{times_tuple_intro,powerset_bottom,empty_generated_well_ordering,fst_eq,snd_eq}.
    Thus $G$ is inhabited.
    For all $p\in G$ we have $\snd{p}$ is a well ordering generated by $f$ on $\fst{p}$.

    Let $U=\{\fst{p}\mid p\in G\}$.
    Let $V=\{\snd{p}\mid p\in G\}$.
    Let $B=\unions{U}$.
    Let $S=\unions{V}$.
    $S$ is a well ordering generated by $f$ on $B$ by \cref{union_generated_well_orderings}.

    Show $B\subseteq A$.
    \begin{subproof}
        Show that for all $C\in U$ we have $C\subseteq A$.
        \begin{subproof}
            Fix $C\in U$.
            Take $p\in G$ such that $C=\fst{p}$.
            $p\in\pow{A}\times\pow{A\times A}$.
            Hence $\fst{p}\in\pow{A}$ by \cref{fst_type}.
            Thus $C\subseteq A$ by \cref{pow_iff}.
        \end{subproof}
        Follows by \cref{unions_family}.
    \end{subproof}

    Show $B=A$.
    \begin{subproof}
        Suppose not.
        $B\in\pow{A}$ by \cref{pow_iff}.
        Hence $B\in H$.
        Let $c=f(B)$.
        $c\in A\setminus B$.
        Hence $c\in A$ and $c\notin B$ by \cref{setminus}.
        Let $C=\cons{c}{B}$.
        $C\subseteq A$ by \cref{cons_subseteq_intro}.
        Hence $C\in\pow{A}$ by \cref{pow_iff}.
        Let $R=S\union(B\times\{c\})$.
        $R$ is a well ordering generated by $f$ on $C$
            by \cref{generated_well_ordering_adjoin}.
        $R\subseteq C\times C$ by \cref{generated_well_ordering,foundational_ordering}.
        $C\times C\subseteq A\times A$
            by \cref{times_subseteq_left,times_subseteq_right,subseteq_transitive}.
        Hence $R\subseteq A\times A$ by \cref{subseteq_transitive}.
        Thus $R\in\pow{A\times A}$ by \cref{pow_iff}.
        $(C,R)\in\pow{A}\times\pow{A\times A}$ by \cref{times_tuple_intro}.
        $\fst{(C,R)}=C$ and $\snd{(C,R)}=R$ by \cref{fst_eq,snd_eq}.
        Hence $(C,R)\in G$.
        Thus $C\in U$.
        Hence $C\subseteq B$ by \cref{subseteq,unions_iff}.
        Therefore $c\in B$ by \cref{cons_left,subseteq}.
        Contradiction.
    \end{subproof}

    Hence $S$ is a foundational ordering on $A$
        by \cref{generated_well_ordering}.
    Thus $S$ is a simple order relation on $A$
        by \cref{foundational_ordering,simple_order_relation}.
    Show that for all $C$ such that $C\subseteq A$ and $C\neq\emptyset$
        there exists $c$ such that $c$ is a smallest element of $C$ with respect to $S$.
    \begin{subproof}
        Fix $C$.
        Assume $C\subseteq A$ and $C\neq\emptyset$.
        Take $c\in C$ such that for all $x\in C$ we have $c\mathrel{S}x$ or $c=x$
            by \cref{foundational_ordering}.
        $c$ is a smallest element of $C$ with respect to $S$
            by \cref{smallest_element}.
        Follows by assumption.
    \end{subproof}
    Hence $S$ is a well order relation on $A$ by \cref{well_order_relation}.
    Follows by assumption.
\end{proof}
